Code: python
def calculate_tyre_dimensions(tyre_code):
    
    import schema
    it = [
        ('iso', _re_tyre_code_iso),
        ('numeric', _re_tyre_code_numeric),
        ('pax', _re_tyre_code_pax)
    ]
    for c, _r in it:
        try:
            m = _r.match(tyre_code).groupdict()
            m['code'] = c
            if c == 'numeric' and 'aspect_ratio' not in m:
                b = m['nominal_section_width'].split('.')[-1][-1] == '5'
                m['aspect_ratio'] = '82' if b else '92'
            return _format_tyre_dimensions(m)
        except (AttributeError, schema.SchemaError):
            pass
    raise ValueError('Invalid tyre code: %s', tyre_code)
Inputs: '205-640 R 440 A 94 T (94 V, 97 H)'

Let's parse code:
```
def calculate_tyre_dimensions(tyre_code):
    import schema
    it = [
        ('iso', _re_tyre_code_iso),
        ('numeric', _re_tyre_code_numeric),
        ('pax', _re_tyre_code_pax)
    ]
    for c, _r in it:
        try:
            m = _r.match(tyre_code).groupdict()
            m['code'] = c
            if c == 'numeric' and 'aspect_ratio' not in m:
                b = m['nominal_section_width'].split('.')[-1][-1] == '5'
                m['aspect_ratio'] = '82' if b else '92'
            return _format_tyre_dimensions(m)
        except (AttributeError, schema.SchemaError):
            pass
    raise ValueError('Invalid tyre code: %s', tyre_code)
```
It tries match from start? `.match` anchors at beginning. If regex doesn't match, m = None, then m.groupdict() raises AttributeError, caught. If matches but schema validation fails, SchemaError caught. Returns formatted.

Let's search memory of library "tyre" source. I can try to recall from Python package "tyre" (maybe on PyPI "tyre"). The code snippet exactly:
```
def calculate_tyre_dimensions(tyre_code):
    import schema
    it = [
        ('iso', _re_tyre_code_iso),
        ('numeric', _re_tyre_code_numeric),
        ('pax', _re_tyre_code_pax)
    ]
    for c, _r in it:
        try:
            m = _r.match(tyre_code).groupdict()
            m['code'] = c
            if c == 'numeric' and 'aspect_ratio' not in m:
                b = m['nominal_section_width'].split('.')[-1][-1] == '5'
                m['aspect_ratio'] = '82' if b else '92'
            return _format_tyre_dimensions(m)
        except (AttributeError, schema.SchemaError):
            pass
    raise ValueError('Invalid tyre code: %s', tyre_code)
```
This might be from "tyre" package by "simonpercivall"? Let's google memory: There is a Python library "tyre" with module "tyre.py" maybe. I might have seen code on StackOverflow? Hmm.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

>>> calculate_tyre_dimensions('6.50-16')

```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({

        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. ```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```

_RE_TYRE_CODE_PAX = re.compile(r"""

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))

        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),

    }).validate(m)

That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

What about "A" in input? It might be ignored or included as `pax` marker. The output might not include "A" except in `tyre_code`.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),

    }).validate(m)

_RE_TYRE_CODE_PAX = re.compile(r"""

        -

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

    if m['code'] == 'pax':

        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),

        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))

        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

_RE_TYRE_CODE_PAX = re.compile(r"""

        -

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))

        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

    ^(?P<tyre_code>
        (?P<nominal_section_width>\d{3})
        -
        (?P<overall_diameter>\d{3})
        \s+
        (?P<construction_type>[A-Z])
        \s+
        (?P<rim_diameter_code>\d{3})
        \s+
        A
        \s+
        (?P<load_index>\d{2})
        (?P<speed_symbol>[A-Z])
        (?:\s+\((?P<alternatives>.*)\))?
    )$
""", re.VERBOSE)

Maybe.

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

What about "A" in input? It might be ignored or included as `pax` marker. The output might not include "A" except in `tyre_code`.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))

        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))

        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```

(?P<tyre_code>
  (?P<nominal_section_width>\d{3})

  (?P<overall_diameter>\d{3})
  \s+

  \s+

  \s+
  A
  \s+
  (?P<load_index>\d{2})

)

```
_RE_TYRE_CODE_PAX = re.compile(r"""
    ^(?P<tyre_code>
        (?P<nominal_section_width>\d{3})
        -
        (?P<overall_diameter>\d{3})
        \s+
        (?P<construction_type>[A-Z])
        \s+
        (?P<rim_diameter_code>\d{3})
        \s+
        A
        \s+
        (?P<load_index>\d{2})
        (?P<speed_symbol>[A-Z])
        (?:\s+\((?P<alternatives>.*)\))?
    )$
""", re.VERBOSE)
```

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

What about "A" in input? It might be ignored or included as `pax` marker. The output might not include "A" except in `tyre_code`.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.

def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))

        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)

Then `_format_tyre_dimensions(m)` might compute `aspect_ratio` from `overall_diameter` and `rim_diameter_code`:
`aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / nominal_section_width * 100)`? For 205-640 R 440: (640-440)/205 = 200/205 = 0.9756 -> 97.56 -> 98? But PAX tyres are low profile, so 98 is wrong. Wait 640 overall diameter, 440 rim diameter, section height = (640-440)/2 = 100 mm. Aspect ratio = 100/205*100 = 48.78 -> 49. Yes, formula uses half difference. So `aspect_ratio = round((overall_diameter - rim_diameter_code) / (2 * nominal_section_width) * 100)`. That gives 49. That seems plausible for PAX (e.g., 245-680 R 440: (680-440)/(2*245)=240/490=48.98 -> 49). PAX tyres have aspect ratio around 50. So `_format_tyre_dimensions` likely computes aspect_ratio for pax using that formula.

The function name "calculate_tyre_dimensions" suggests it calculates aspect ratio for PAX and numeric. For numeric, aspect ratio is derived from width ending in 5 (82 or 92) because numeric codes don't have aspect ratio. For PAX, aspect ratio is calculated from overall diameter and rim diameter. So output likely includes `aspect_ratio` = 49.

Let's search memory of `_format_tyre_dimensions` maybe:
```
def _format_tyre_dimensions(m):
    if m['code'] == 'pax':
        m['aspect_ratio'] = str(int(round((int(m['overall_diameter']) - int(m['rim_diameter_code'])) / (2 * int(m['nominal_section_width'])) * 100)))
    return schema.Schema({
        'tyre_code': str,
        'nominal_section_width': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'aspect_ratio': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'construction_type': schema.Or(str, None),
        'rim_diameter_code': schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)),
        'load_index': schema.Or(schema.And(str, schema.Use(int)), None),
        'speed_symbol': schema.Or(str, None),
    }).validate(m)
```
But for PAX, there is `overall_diameter` too. Maybe output includes `overall_diameter`.
